A critical engineering differentiator for the LX40FI was the "Standard Heavy" cycle, which provided a $70^{\circ}F$ rise in temperature, allowing for an incoming water temperature of only $110^{\circ}F$. This was achieved through high-wattage electric booster heaters that could be field-configured for specific facility requirements.FeatureLX30/LX30FILX40/LX40FILX18 (Cold/Hot)Water Consumption (gal/rack)1.21.2 (2.2 in Heavy)2.6 - 5.2Sanitizing AgentChlorine or Hot WaterChlorine or Hot WaterChlorine or Hot WaterIncoming Water Temp (min)$140^{\circ}F$$140^{\circ}F$ ($110^{\circ}F$ in FI)$140^{\circ}F$ (Hot)Tank MaterialStainless SteelStainless SteelStainless SteelML Number Examples104352, 104354104355, 104356104349, 104351Source: The mechanical complexity of these legacy units, particularly in the chemical pump assemblies and the "ratio fill" valves, necessitated significant service expertise. Modern Hobart service manuals emphasize that the transition to the LXi and LXe series streamlined these components into solid-state control boards.Contemporary LXe and LXn Advansys ArchitecturesThe LXe series introduced the "SmartDisplay," a digital interface that replaced mechanical timers with microprocessor-driven cycle management. The Advansys LXeR model further advanced undercounter technology by integrating steam elimination and energy recovery. The current LXn series builds upon this with a two-level rack system, effectively doubling the machine’s capacity without increasing its footprint.FeatureLXeR (Advansys)LXnH (Base High Temp)LXnC (Base Low Temp)Racks per Hour13 (H) / 24 (N) / 30 (L)32 (Light)34 (Light)Capacity with 2L Rack48 Racks/Hr48 Racks/Hr48 Racks/HrWater Consumption0.62 Gallons/Rack0.62 Gallons/Cycle0.62 Gallons/CycleSanitization Method180°F Hot Water180°F Hot Water8.4% BleachSteam EliminationIntegratedN/AN/AWiFi/SmartConnectStandardStandardStandardSource: The hydraulic engineering of the LXn series utilizes "PuriRinse" technology in low-temperature models. PuriRinse is a cold-water final rinse that removes chemical residues left by sodium hypochlorite, ensuring that glassware does not retain off-tastes or chemical odors. The LXn also features a "Deluxe Strainer System" and interchangeable wash/rinse arms that utilize a tool-free "snap-in" design for rapid service and cleaning.Door-Type Platforms: AM14, AM15, and AM16 EngineeringHobart’s door-type dishwashers, specifically the AM series, are designed for high-intensity commercial kitchens. The technical architecture of these machines focuses on heavy-duty pump performance and rapid thermal cycling.Legacy AM14 and AM15 Technical AnalysisThe AM15 model served as a benchmark for door-type engineering for over a decade. It utilized a 2 HP wash motor delivering a pump capacity of 160 GPM. The hydraulic circuit featured a "Ratio Fill" method, which used a microcomputer-controlled fill valve to ensure precise water volumes regardless of fluctuating line pressures.ComponentAM15 (Hot Water)AM15 (Chemical)Wash Cycle Time38 Seconds38 SecondsDwell Time2 Seconds2 SecondsRinse Time10 Seconds10 SecondsSani-Dwell Time7 SecondsN/ARacks per Hour5865Wash Tank Capacity14 Gallons14 GallonsWash Pump Flow Rate160 GPM160 GPMSource: The AM15 was also notable for its versatile heating options. While electric immersion elements (5 kW) were standard, Hobart offered an optional gas-heated version using a 25,000 BTU/Hr infrared immersion tube system. The gas variant required a solid-state igniter board and a step-down transformer to 24 volts for the gas valves.AM16 Advansys and AMX Series Global VariantsThe transition to the AM16 series brought the Advansys technology suite to the door-type platform. The AM16 Advansys (ADV) model includes ASR, DWER, and a ventless option that uses an internal condensing system to eliminate the need for an exhaust hood. The AM16 also introduced "X-shaped" wash arms, which are designed to improve water distribution and strip heavy food soils.The AMX and AMXX series, prevalent in international and European markets, feature the "Visiotronic-Touch" control system. This system utilizes a color touchscreen to provide graphical representations of cycle progress and diagnostic data. A key engineering feature of the AMX is the "Exhaust Energy Storage" four-sided hood, which traps steam and heat within the chamber, reducing the total energy load by up to 3 kW per hour.Technical SpecificationAM16 Advansys (ADV)AMX (Basic)AMX-S (Water Softener)Rinse Water Consumption0.67 gal/rack2.0 L/rack2.0 L/rackTank Heating Load5 kW2.5 kW2.5 kWBooster Heating Load7.1 kW6.1 kW6.1 kWWash Pump Rating2 HP0.7 kW0.7 kWNoise Emission LevelN/A63 dB(A)63 dB(A)Loading Height (Door)17" or 27" (Tall)440 mm440 mmSource: Rack Conveyor Engineering: The CL SeriesThe CL series represents the peak of high-volume warewashing technology, capable of processing between 202 and 342 racks per hour. These machines are modular, allowing for varied tank configurations (CL44, CL54, CL64) and heating sources (Electric, Steam, or Gas).CL Series Hydraulic and Conveyor MechanicsThe CL conveyor system utilizes a "Rapid Return" drive mechanism powered by a 1/6 HP motor. This mechanism is designed to move racks smoothly through the machine without the jerking motion associated with older chain-drive systems. The hydraulic system is centered on the "Opti-Rinse" spray pattern, which uses precision nozzles to deliver large water droplets. This engineering choice is based on the principle that larger droplets retain thermal energy longer than a fine mist, resulting in more effective heat transfer for sanitization.ParameterCL44e (Electric)CL54e (Steam)CL64e (Advansys)Racks per Hour (NSF)202245342Conveyor Speed (FPM)5.66.89.5Wash Tank Capacity (gal)232323Power Rinse Tank (gal)N/AN/A22Water Usage (gal/rack)0.450.560.39Peak Drain Flow (GPM)383838Source: Electrical Engineering and Single-Point ConnectionsOne of the significant technical challenges in conveyor design is managing the electrical load of multiple pumps and large booster heaters. Hobart has engineered the CL series to support "Single Point Service Connections". For example, the CL44-ADV requires a 208/60/3 connection with a rated amperage of 109.6 to power the 15 kW tank heat, the 18 kW booster, and all motors. For facilities with restricted amperage, Hobart offers "Dual Point Connection" kits that separate the motor/control circuits from the heating elements.Voltage/PhaseCL44e Rated AmpsCL44e Min CircuitCL64e Rated AmpsCL64e Min Circuit208/60/3109.6150115.7150240/60/399.9125105.1125480/60/351.76054.770600/60/339.15043.460Source: Flight-Type Systems: FT1000e Series ArchitectureThe FT1000e series is designed for ultra-high-volume environments, such as stadiums or large institutional cafeterias. These machines are massive, modular assemblies that can wash up to 14,310 dishes per hour while utilizing less water and energy than any competing platform.Thermodynamic Heat Pump and Ventless TechnologyThe engineering centerpiece of the FT1000e Advansys is its integrated heat pump system. This system captures the humid, hot air from the exhaust and uses it to pre-heat the incoming cold water and the machine's blower dryer. This recirculating loop is so efficient that it eliminates the need for a vent hood in many installations, saving thousands in HVAC infrastructure costs. The FT1000e series is also designed with the "Lowest Connected Load" in the industry at 150 amps, which avoids the need for massive electrical upgrades in existing facilities.SectionComponent TypeSpecificationPre-Wash PumpTEFC Motor3.5 HPPower Wash PumpTEFC Motor5.0 HPPower Rinse PumpTEFC Motor5.0 HPConveyor DriveVariable Speed1/3 HPBlower DryerDual Fan(2) 2.0 HP MotorsConveyor SpeedKeypad Adjusted4.0 - 8.5 FPMSource: Modular Assembly and Field InstallationInstalling an FT1000e requires meticulous mechanical alignment. The machine is shipped in sections that must be leveled end-to-end and front-to-back using stainless steel adjustable feet. A specialized engineering feature is the ability to reduce the control box height by 4.5 inches for move-in through low-clearance corridors by shifting mounting studs to secondary slots on the shipping braces. Once the sections are mated using vinyl foam tape and 5/16-18 mounting hardware, the conveyor links must be run continuously for 15 minutes to verify alignment before final torqueing.Prep Washers: PW and PWn EngineeringPrep washers are engineered to clean high-soil items like mixing bowls, dough troughs, and sheet pans. Advansys models in this category include "Sense-A-Temp" technology to ensure $180^{\circ}F$ sanitization and "Booster Guard," which protects the heating elements from scale buildup.Control Board and Digital Logic SystemsHobart’s modern equipment relies on complex PCB (Printed Circuit Board) architectures to manage timing, safety interlocks, and sensor data.PCB Components and Part NumbersThe control system typically consists of a master control board, a relay board, and a user interface (HMI) board. For the AM15 and AM16 series, the master control board (e.g., 00-897545-00001 or 00-941944-00006) manages the high-voltage relay signals and interprets data from thermistors and pressure transducers.Part NumberTypeCompatible ModelsDimensions (approx)00-897545-00001Control BoardAM16, CL Series8.15" x 5.95"00-892932-00002Control Board AssyAM15, LXe, LXG7.5" x 7.75"00-938408Board PC KeyboardLXe, AM16, CLUser Interface00-974835Control BoardCLe SeriesMain Logic294457EControl BoardLegacy AM/LXiMain Logic403.986Control PCBEuropean/Global185mm x 45mmSource: Sequence of Operations and Interlock LogicThe firmware in Hobart control boards follows a rigid sequence of operations to ensure food safety and equipment protection. For the AM16 series, the sequence begins with the "Machine Off" state. Upon pressing the "On" key, the machine initiates a "Fill/Preheat" cycle.Booster Filling: The system monitors the pressure transmitter (B3/B4) or float switches. If the booster is empty, the software inhibits the heating elements to prevent "dry firing," which would destroy the incoloy elements.Wash Cycle Initiation: Triggered by the closing of the door interlock switch (LS1). The control board energizes the coil of contactor CON3, which provides line voltage to the wash pump motor (MTR1). In chemical-sanitizing models, the detergent pump (MTR7) is simultaneously energized via an extension card.Dwell and Rinse: After the wash timer (e.g., 38 seconds) expires, a 2-second dwell permits the upper manifold to drain. The final rinse solenoid valve is then energized, provided the booster sensor (QTM3) confirms a temperature $\ge 180^{\circ}F$.ASR and Condense: In Advansys models, the ASR pump (MTR2) and the condensing fan (MTR4) are sequenced post-rinse to manage humidity and soil.Diagnostics and Error Code MatricesHobart machines use a dual-diagnostic system: numeric codes for internal processing and plain-language or error codes for the operator.Comprehensive Error Code AnalysisThe following matrix details the engineering conditions that trigger common Hobart error codes.CodeModel SeriesMeaningDiagnostic Trigger/ThresholdE0, E2, E3LXi, AM SelectFill TimeoutSump level not sensed within 150-300 secondsE5LXi, AM SelectDrain TimeoutSump level does not drop below threshold during drainP1, P2, P3AM, LXeSensor ErrorThermistor resistance $>150k \Omega$ (open) or $<5k \Omega$ (short)Error 023AM16 AdvansysRapid Fill ErrorWater level did not increase by 0.1V in 150 secondsError 033AM16 AdvansysStrainer MissingReed sensor LS2 input not sensed for $\ge 1$ secondError 050CL SeriesRinse CalibrationBooster pressure sensor AI3 $<0.44V$ or $>0.56V$Error 081CL, AM16Booster OvertempFinal rinse sensor QTM3 $\ge 239^{\circ}F$Error 160CL SeriesTank Temp LowTank sensor QTM2 $\le$ Setpoint $- 18^{\circ}F$ for 2 minsError 501FT1000eTank 1 Low TempResistance $> 15k \Omega$ on Tank 1 thermistorError 513FT1000eTank D Probe ErrResistance $< 5k \Omega$ on Dual Rinse sensorSource: Advanced Diagnostic ProceduresFor service professionals, the "Analog Inputs" display mode allows for real-time monitoring of sensor voltages. When troubleshooting a low-temperature rinse, the technician must verify the status of digital output DO7 (Rinse Pump) and the continuity of the CON1 contactor coil. In "Manual Delime" mode for the FT1000e, the technician must set the "Auto Timeout" parameter to 30 minutes and manually disable the detergent feed via the manager menu (default PIN 1001) to allow the chemical to work without dilution.Wiring Logic and Schematic ArchitectureHobart’s wiring diagrams are critical for resolving complex electrical faults. The architecture has shifted from traditional point-to-point wiring to "Single Plane" circuitry in the FT1000e, which mounts all controls in an eye-level center for accessibility.AM16 and LXn Wiring CharacteristicsFor the AM16 series, diagrams are differentiated by serial number. Post-serial number 231270380, the machines utilize a standardized 208-240/60/3 or 1ph connection where line voltage is applied to DPS terminals TB3.3 and TB3.4. The interlock switches (LS1 for door, LS2 for strainer) are typically low-voltage (24VDC) circuits that signal the microprocessor to enable or inhibit the power contactors.The LXn series undercounters incorporate chemical pumps as standard equipment. The wiring for these pumps (MTR4 for Detergent, MTR5 for Rinse Aid, MTR6 for Sanitizer) is routed through the main control board, which uses Pulse Width Modulation (PWM) to calibrate chemical dosages based on the selected wash program (Light, Normal, or Heavy).Maintenance Protocols and Preventive EngineeringThe longevity of Hobart warewashers is predicated on the management of lime scale and the integrity of mechanical seals.Deliming and Chemical Management"Booster Guard" technology in Advansys models represents an automated solution to the problem of lime scale. In standard machines, the booster heater is difficult to access, and scale buildup can lead to element failure or poor heat transfer. The Booster Guard system automatically dispenses delimer into the booster heater during the delime cycle, ensuring that the internal tank is cleaned alongside the main wash chamber.Maintenance TaskFrequencyRequirementsManual CleaningEvery ShiftFlush screens, remove wash armsDeliming CyclePer Machine PromptDelimer chemical, manual or auto doseConveyor LubricationBiannuallyH2 Food Grade Lithium GreaseCheck Wash Arm SlidersDailyMust be in "Closed" positionBaffle Cleaning6-12 MonthsRemove and clear clogs in ventless unitsSource: Lubrication and Drive System MaintenanceIn flight-type machines like the FT1000e, the conveyor drive platform includes a load-sensing switch that provides jam protection. If a rack or piece of silverware jams the belt, the increased load on the 1/3 HP drive motor is sensed, and the power is cut to prevent mechanical damage. Service technicians must check the "Anti-Jam Switch Adjustment" periodically to ensure the sensitivity is correctly calibrated.Hydraulic Physics: The "Hobart Clean" StandardThe effectiveness of Hobart equipment is not just a function of cycle time, but of the specific interaction between water temperature and pressure.Kinetic Energy in Wash SystemsThe 2 HP pumps used in the AM and CL series are engineered to maximize GPM over PSI. While high pressure might seem ideal, excessive pressure can lead to "shingling" (where lightweight items like plates move and block each other). Hobart’s revolving stainless steel wash arms use unrestricted openings to provide a "flooding" action, which ensures that all surfaces, including those in the corners of the rack, are saturated.Thermal Conductivity and SanitizationIn hot water sanitizing models, the final rinse must provide a minimum of $180^{\circ}F$ water at 20 PSI flowing pressure. Hobart’s "Sense-A-Temp" technology acts as a thermal interlock; the final rinse valve will not open if the booster heater cannot confirm the water is at the correct temperature. This ensures that every rack leaves the machine sanitized to NSF standards, regardless of the incoming water temperature or the frequency of use.Engineering Reliability and Material SpecificationsThe reliability of Hobart dishwashers is deeply rooted in the materials selected for their construction. The use of stainless steel 1.4301 (AISI 304) for all tanks, frames, and panels provides superior resistance to the corrosive effects of commercial detergents and high-temperature water.Pump Seals: Hobart uses carbon-ceramic sealing interfaces with stainless steel springs to handle the high-speed rotation and thermal fluctuations within the wash pump.Impeller Design: Stainless steel impellers are integrated directly with the motor shafts to ensure perfect alignment and reduce vibration, which is a primary cause of mechanical seal failure in commercial pumps.Modular Wiring: The use of $105^{\circ}C$, 600V thermoplastic insulated wire with stranded conductors ensures that the electrical system can withstand the internal temperatures of the machine without insulation degradation.In summary, the Hobart commercial dishwashing ecosystem is defined by a modular engineering philosophy that prioritizes resource efficiency through heat recovery and soil management. By analyzing the detailed technical specifications of models ranging from the compact LXn to the massive FT1000e, it is clear that Hobart has standardized a suite of technologies—Advansys, Sense-A-Temp, and SmartConnect—that provide a consistent engineering framework across the entire product line. This technical compendium serves as the foundation for an engineering knowledge base, providing the structured data and service insights necessary for the professional maintenance and operation of Hobart equipment.

-Technical Engineering Knowledge Base: Hobart Commercial Warewashing Systems
The architectural and mechanical engineering of commercial warewashing equipment has undergone significant evolution since the acquisition of the Crescent company by Hobart in 1926. This technical repository serves as a comprehensive knowledge base for Hobart Corporation's dishwashing systems, detailing the structural, hydraulic, electrical, and control parameters across legacy and current production models. The following analysis prioritizes official manufacturer data, service documentation, and engineering specifications to provide a nuanced understanding of the equipment's internal mechanisms and operational logic.Technical Taxonomy and Product NomenclatureHobart’s descriptive nomenclature system provides an engineering shorthand for machine classification, performance tiering, and integrated hardware features. This system is critical for specifying the correct infrastructure during facility design and for identifying field revision levels during service interventions.Model Type ClassificationThe primary character of a Hobart model number indicates its structural configuration and primary application. A 'G' designation identifies dedicated glasswashers, typically engineered with lower-velocity spray patterns to protect delicate stemware. Front-loading machines are denoted by 'F', providing a compact footprint for undercounter installations. Utensil washers, designed for high-pressure mechanical removal of baked-on soils, use the 'U' prefix. Hood-type or door-style machines are identified by 'AM' (traditional hood) or 'AU' (high-pressure hood), where the 'H' suffix often distinguishes a standard vertical hood lift.Performance and Feature TieringSecondary alphanumeric identifiers categorize the equipment’s technical sophistication and hydraulic equipment:X (PROFI): Indicates a full equipment package comprising the GENIUS-X² fine filter system, integrated drain and rinse pumps, and automated chemical dispensing technology.P (PREMAX): Signifies the premium tier, often including patented innovations like steam management, integrated drying, or the lowest water consumption parameters available.XX: Specifically denotes the ROTOR-X wash system, an engineering advancement in spray arm geometry that increases water distribution efficiency.S (Softener): Indicates an integrated water softening module, utilizing ion-exchange resin to mitigate calcium carbonate buildup.L (Large): Refers to an expanded wash chamber, often with a 27" opening to accommodate standard 18" x 26" sheet pans or 60-quart mixing bowls.R (Heat Recovery): Denotes the inclusion of energy recovery systems, such as heat exchangers that capture energy from waste steam or drain water.T (Twin): Identifies double-width machines capable of processing two racks simultaneously.IdentifierTechnical CategoryMechanical ContextGGlasswasherRinse-pressure optimized for optics and head retentionFFront-LoadingUndercounter architecture; 0.85 HP typical pumpAMHood-TypeDoor-actuated start; spring-counterbalanced chamberAUHigh-Pressure HoodEnhanced GPM for heavy soil applicationsXPROFIStandardizes GENIUS-X² filtration and integrated pumpsPPREMAXIncludes Energy Recovery and Advanced Cycle logicXXROTOR-XSpecialized 4-arm or X-arm wash manifold geometrySSoftenerIntegrated brine-tank and regeneration logicREnergy RecoveryVentless heat-exchange technologyUndercounter Dishwashing Architecture: LXe and LXi SeriesThe transition from the legacy LXi series to the current LXe and LXn generations represents a significant shift in control logic and thermal efficiency. While the LXi provided a foundation of dependable performance, the LXe and subsequent Advansys models introduced sophisticated diagnostic sensors and energy-recapture mechanisms.LXe and Advansys LXe Engineering ParametersThe LXe series is divided into Base (LXeH/C) and Advansys (LXeR/PR) models. The primary architectural objective of the LXeR is "Energy Recovery," which enables ventless operation by condensing steam into a usable energy source.Hydraulic and Thermal SpecificationsThe LXeR high-temperature model utilizes Hobart's patented Energy Recovery technology. In this system, hot water vapor produced during the wash and rinse cycles is channeled through an evaporator coil. The latent heat is used to preheat the incoming cold water line to approximately 110°F before it enters the Sense-A-Temp booster heater.ParameterLXeH (Base High Temp)LXeR (Advansys High Temp)LXeC (Base Low Temp)LXePR (Advansys Low Temp)Racks/Hour (Normal)32303438Water Use (Gal/Rack)0.740.620.741.14Wash Tank Capacity2.9 Gallons2.9 Gallons2.9 Gallons2.9 GallonsWash Motor0.85 HP0.85 HP0.85 HP0.85 HPBooster Rise70°F (Sense-A-Temp)70°F (Sense-A-Temp)N/AN/ASanitizationHot Water (180°F)Hot Water (180°F)Chemical (120°F-140°F)Chemical (120°F-140°F)The Advansys LXePR low-temperature model introduces the "PuriRinse" feature. This engineering intervention addresses chemical residue issues inherent in low-temp machines. After the chemical sanitizer cycle, the system provides a fresh-water rinse, which was made possible through Hobart’s collaboration with the FDA and EPA to amend the Food Code.Control Logic and Diagnostic IndicatorsThe LXe control interface moved from the LXi's simple alphanumeric codes to a descriptive "Smart Display". This system utilizes microcomputer-based logic to monitor a wider range of sensory inputs.Sump Level Sensor Fault: Indicates that the pressure switch for the wash tank is out of range. This can be caused by a blocked air trap or a failure in the pressure transducer on the main motherboard.Wash Arm Alert: Triggered when the machine detects that the wash arm is not rotating at the correct RPM. This is often due to physical obstruction from a utensil or mineral buildup in the nozzles.Booster Temp Sensor: Monitored to ensure the 180°F rinse threshold is maintained for sanitization compliance. If the sensor detects a drift, it flags a "Low Rinse Temp" warning, but allows the machine to continue operation for up to 10 cycles before requiring a service reset.Hood-Type Dishwasher Engineering: AM Series EvolutionThe Hobart AM series (specifically the AM15 Select and AM16) serves as the primary high-throughput solution for mid-sized commercial kitchens. The technical revision from AM15 to AM16 introduced several architectural changes, including a pillarless hood and integrated connectivity.Structural and Mechanical Revision: AM15 to AM16The AM16 series introduced the "pillarless" hood design, which utilizes a 3-sided chamber with UHMW polyethylene guides and a spring-counterbalanced lift mechanism. This configuration eliminates corner posts, providing unobstructed access for straight-through or corner installations.FeatureAM15 Select (Legacy)AM16 Base (Current)Throughput (Racks/Hr)58 (Hot) / 65 (Chem)60Wash Pump Motor1.5 HP2.0 HP (High Efficiency)Wash Tank Capacity14 Gallons10.5 GallonsFinal Rinse Volume0.74 Gal/Rack0.67 Gal/RackOpening Height17" (Standard)17" (Base) / 27" (Tall)Control SystemSolid State DigitalTouchscreen WiFi-EnabledAM16 Hydraulic ArchitectureThe AM16 hydraulic system is powered by a 2.0 HP self-draining wash pump featuring a stainless steel impeller. The self-draining design is critical for hygiene, as it ensures that the pump housing does not retain stagnant water during downtime. The pump flow is distributed through revolving, interchangeable "X-shaped" upper and lower anti-clogging wash arms.For high-temperature sanitization, the Sense-A-Temp integrated booster heater provides a 70°F rise, bringing 110°F inlet water to the required 180°F rinse temperature. The rinse cycle is managed by a dedicated 0.18 HP rinse pump, which ensures consistent pressure regardless of fluctuations in the facility's water supply.Electrical and Control InfrastructureThe AM16 series standardizes a single-point electrical connection, although it is field-convertible to dual-point for installations requiring separate circuits for the booster and the wash motors. The machine is 3-phase standard but convertible to single-phase.The control system features integrated WiFi and the SmartConnect app, which logs temperature data, error codes, and usage statistics. Firmware updates for the AM16 are delivered as .hob or .bin files and can be loaded via the service menu to adjust cycle parameters.Commissioning and Motor RotationOn 480V and 50Hz machines, verifying the wash pump motor rotation is a mandatory step. The technician must observe the motor fan to ensure it matches the rotation arrow on the housing. If the rotation is reversed, two incoming power leads must be interchanged at the terminal block.Rack Conveyor Systems: CL Series Technical ArchitectureThe CL (C-Line) series, comprising models like the CL44e and CL66e, is engineered for high-volume automated processing. These machines utilize a modular zone-based approach, where racks move through scrap, wash, and rinse sections.Conveyor Drive and Speed LogicThe conveyor system is driven by a 1/6 HP motor utilizing a "Rapid Return" drive mechanism. This mechanism is coupled with a patented ball detent clutch to protect against conveyor jams. The conveyor speed is typically 5.6 feet per minute, yielding a throughput of 202 racks per hour.Hydraulic Design and Opti-Rinse TechnologyThe CL series utilizes the "Opti-Rinse" system, which significantly reduces water consumption by using a specific spray pattern that generates larger droplets. These droplets are more effective at transferring thermal energy to the ware than a fine mist, allowing the machine to meet NSF sanitization standards with as little as 0.62 gallons per rack.ComponentEngineering SpecificationFunctional NoteWash Tank23 Gallon CapacityStainless steel drawn tankWash Pump2.0 HP; 165 GPMWeir test capacityDrive Motor1/6 HPRapid return with jam protectionBooster Heater30 kW (Built-in)Pressure-less stainless steelASR Motor1/2 HPAutomatic soil removal systemDrain Rate38 GPMPeak initial flowAutomatic Soil Removal (ASR)In Advansys CL models, the ASR system is a standard feature. A specialized 1/2 HP pump redirects food soil from the wash tank to an external scrap basket at the load end of the machine. This system reduces the frequency of water changes by up to 50% and maintains the potency of chemical detergents by preventing soil saturation.Drain Water Energy Recovery (DWER)The DWER system in Advansys models captures heat from the 160°F wash water as it is drained. This thermal energy is transferred to the incoming cold water line (P3) specifically used for the final rinse. The P3 cold water connection must be maintained at a maximum of 80°F to ensure effective heat transfer.Electrical Legend and Connection PointsThe CL series requires precise electrical termination at heights specified "Above Finished Floor" (AFF).E1 (Single Point): 65" AFF; services motors, controls, tank heat, and the 30kW booster heater.E2 (Dual Point - Machine): 62" AFF; services motors and tank heat.E3 (Dual Point - Booster): 65" AFF; services controls and the booster heater.P1 (Drain): 2" FPT connection; 7-3/8" AFF.P2 (Hot Water): 1/2" FPT connection; 11-3/16" or 12" AFF.Flight-Type Machine Engineering: FT1000 SeriesThe FT1000 series represents Hobart's highest-capacity platform, designed for continuous loading in large institutions. These machines are modular tunnel systems that can exceed 21 feet in length.Sectional Architecture and Water FlowThe FT1000 is divided into functional chambers, each with a dedicated pump and motor assembly.ASR Section: 2-foot section with specialized soil removal arms and a dedicated pump.Prewash Chamber: 40-gallon tank; powered by a 3-1/2 or 4 HP TEFC motor.Power Wash Chamber: 40-gallon tank; powered by a 4 or 5 HP TEFC motor.Power Rinse Chamber: 32 or 40-gallon tank; powered by a 3-1/2 or 5 HP TEFC motor.Dual Rinse/Final Rinse: Pumped rinse system with 6.5 to 7.2-gallon dual-rinse tank.SpecificationFT1000 Energy RecoveryFT1000i (Airline)Water Consumption58 GPH78 GPHConveyor Width30"30" (Airline Laned)Drive Motor1/3 HP1/3 HPExhaust (Unload)750 CFM750 CFMFinal Rinse Rate1.0 GPM1.3 GPMPeak Drain Flow38 GPM30 GPMVentless Energy Recovery and Heat Pump TechnologyThe FT1000 Advansys employs a ventless energy recovery system that functions as an integrated heat pump. This system captures heat from the exhaust air and uses it to maintain temperatures in the wash tank, blower dryer, and final rinse. This technology eliminates the requirement for a dedicated vent hood, saving thousands of dollars in annual HVAC costs and reducing the total connected load requirement to approximately 150 amps.Hydraulic and Water System EngineeringThe cleaning efficacy of Hobart equipment is a function of the "Cleaning Unit" (CU), derived from the relationship between pressure (PSI) and volume (GPM). Hobart engineering typically prioritizes high volume (GPM) for rinsing to ensure effective flushing of dirt and grime.Pump Mechanics and PerformanceThe recirculating pumps in Hobart dishwashers are engineered for the harsh chemistry and temperatures of a commercial kitchen.Materials: Pumps utilize stainless steel housings and impellers with carbon-ceramic sealing interfaces.Design: Totally Enclosed Fan Cooled (TEFC) motors provide protection against water spray and steam during clean-up.Flow Rates: A standard AM15 or CL44e wash pump is rated for approximately 160-165 GPM via a weir test, providing the high-velocity mechanical action required to strip food soil.Water Requirements and Quality ParametersTo maintain engineering specifications, the building’s water supply must meet several critical parameters.ParameterRecommended ValueEngineering ImpactWater Hardness< 3 Grains/GallonPrevents scale on probes and heatersChloride Concentration< 50 PPMPrevents pitting of stainless motherboard with modular I/O cards for specific peripherals.Motherboard (e.g., 04-006176-001): The core logic center, responsible for processing inputs from temperature thermistors and pressure level (208-480V) to 120V or 24V AC for the control circuitry. These schematics are classified by serial number ranges, as mid-production revisions often involve changes to the wiring harness or relay board.Float Switches: Use low-voltage DC signals. In legacy C-Line and AM15 models, mechanical floats were used for low-water protection to prevent heater dry-firing.Pressure Sensors: In newer CL44e and FT1000 models, durable precision pressure sensor monitors replace mechanical floats to provide more granular water-level feedback.Maintenance and Diagnostic ProtocolsIntegrated diagnostics and automated maintenance routines are designed to preserve the mechanical integrity of the equipment over its operational life.Service Diagnostic CodesThe microcomputer system monitors operational parameters and triggers specific alphanumeric or text alerts when sensors drift out of range.Code / MessageSystemDiagnostic LogicFill ErrorHydraulicFill time exceeds programmed threshold (60-120s)Drain ErrorHydraulicSump pressure transducer detects water after drain cycleE0, E2, E3, E4Hydraulic (LXi)Low water level detected during fillE5Hydraulic (LXi)Water remains in tank after drain cycle completesWash Arm AlertMechanicalRotational sensor mismatch with pump energizationBooster Level SensorControlPressure switch indicates low water in booster tankSump Level SensorControlSump pressure transducer out of rangeLow Rinse TempThermalRinse temperature <180°F for two consecutive cyclesDeliming and Water TreatmentHobart’s "Complete Delime" system with "Booster Guard" is an engineering solution for hard-water management. The system automatically doses a specific amount of deliming agent into the final rinse section and the booster heater during the delime cycle. This maintains the heat transfer efficiency of the electric elements and protects the precision spray nozzles from mineral occlusion.The Advansys water treatment system (WS-80) is recommended for all installations where water hardness exceeds 3 grains. It incorporates high-temp filter housings (PF-10-EHT) and sediment cartridges (SD-510HT) to ensure that only potable, mineral-neutral water enters the machine's hydraulic pathways.Legacy C-Line and Discontinued SystemsMaintaining legacy equipment like the CA-line (C44A, C54A) and the first-generation C-Line (C9, C39) requires an understanding of electromechanical control logic and traditional steam engineering.Mechanical Features of Legacy C-LineLegacy machines utilized atmospheric vacuum breakers on all fill and rinse lines to prevent reverse flow into the potable water supply. Motor protection was provided by physical overload relays housed within the control boxes.Steam and Gas Heating SystemsIn legacy CA-models, steam heat was controlled via solenoid-actuated mechanical globe valves. Steam traps had to be specifically selected based on the flowing pressure (under 20 PSI or 20-45 PSI). Gas-heated legacy units featured natural or LP gas burners (78,000 BTU/Hr) ignited automatically by solid-state circuitry, eliminating the need for a manual pilot light.Unit Conversion and Field SetupLegacy instructions emphasize the importance of checking the data plate specifications against the electrical service before installation. If the machine was equipped with multiple motors, technicians only needed to check the rotation of one, as all were wired at the factory to rotate in the same direction.Structural Integrity and Material ScienceThe structural design of Hobart dishwashers focuses on hygiene and durability through the use of high-grade stainless steel.Drawn Tanks: Wash tanks are deep-drawn from single sheets of stainless steel, eliminating welds and sharp corners where bacteria and soil can accumulate.Capless Wash Arms: Standard on modern CL and FT models, these arms eliminate removable end caps that are frequently lost, simplifying the daily cleaning process for operators.Insulated Cabinets: Door-style and conveyor machines utilize insulated ergonomic cabinet doors to reduce heat dissipation into the kitchen and lower the noise level during operation.Conclusion of Engineering PrinciplesThe engineering framework for Hobart commercial dishwashers is defined by a commitment to resource efficiency and structural longevity. From the high-GPM recirculating wash systems to the advanced ventless energy recovery modules, every component is designed to withstand the rigors of industrial food service while minimizing the environmental footprint. The transition toward microcomputer-controlled systems like the Advansys line has enabled more precise sanitization monitoring, enhanced diagnostic capabilities, and significant labor savings through features like Automatic Soil Removal. This technical knowledge base provides the foundation for the continued engineering, service, and optimization of Hobart's professional warewashing portfolio.

-Engineering Knowledge Base: Hobart Commercial Warewashing Systems
Historical Engineering Evolution and Industrial ContextThe technical heritage of Hobart Corporation’s warewashing division represents a continuous trajectory of hydraulic and mechanical refinement that began with the acquisition of the Crescent Washing Machine Company in 1926. This established a foundation based on the original 1886 patents of Josephine Cochrane, which introduced the fundamental concept of high-pressure water impingement as a substitute for manual scrubbing. Throughout the mid-20th century, Hobart engineering focused on the structural durability of the "Low Boy" designs and the introduction of the FT (Flight-Type) series in 1950, which utilized a continuous conveyor belt for high-volume institutional applications.The transition to modern engineering standards occurred in 1999 when Hobart was integrated into the Illinois Tool Works (ITW) Food Equipment Group. This move catalyzed the development of the Advansys platform, characterized by a shift from electro-mechanical timers to microcomputer-based control systems and the integration of sophisticated energy recovery modules. In 2011, the opening of a dedicated international research and development design center in Elgersweier, Germany, signaled a focus on computational fluid dynamics (CFD) for manifold design and the adoption of heat-pump technology for ventless configurations.Milestone YearEngineering InnovationTechnical Impact1886First Dishwasher Patent (Cochran)Mechanical replacement for manual immersion cleaning.1926Acquisition of Crescent Washing Machine Co.Hobart's entry into the commercial warewashing sector.1953First Flight-Type Dishwasher PatentEnabled continuous high-volume conveyorized cleaning.1999Integration into ITW Food Equipment GroupEnhanced access to electronic control and materials science R&D.2007Launch of PREMAX TechnologyReductions in water (50%), energy (30%), and chemicals (80%).2011New International R&D Center OpeningFocused on CFD-optimized hydraulics and heat-pump modules.2014AUTO-CLEAN and twinLINE TechnologyAutomation of internal sanitation and modular conveyor logic.Product Nomenclature and Architectural TaxonomyThe Hobart nomenclature system is a structured alphanumeric code that provides immediate insight into the unit's mechanical architecture and intended application.Machine Category IndicatorsThe primary prefix indicates the machine's functional form factor:G (Glasswasher): Optimized for low-profile installation and delicate glassware The LXi series utilized 1.0 HP wash motors and a centrifugal-type pump with a capacity of 54 GPM. This increased mechanical power allows for more aggressive cleaning of tough food soils without increasing cycle times. The AM16 Advansys model further incorporates Automatic Soil Removal (ASR), which utilizes a dedicated hydraulic loop to pump food soil out of the wash tank into an external scrap basket, extending the life of the wash water.Structural Safety and Hood DesignModern AM16 units utilize a 5-sided insulated hood, which retains thermal energy and reduces heat gain to the dishroom by 40%. The hood lift mechanism is spring-counterbalanced and guided by UHMW (Ultra-High Molecular Weight) polyethylene rollers to ensure consistent vertical travel over a minimum of 1,000,000 cycles.Hydraulic Engineering: Pumps, Motors, and Mechanical SealsHobart’s hydraulic systems are designed to maximize impingement force—the mechanical energy of water hitting the ware—while minimizing total water consumption.Pump and Motor AssemblyThe recirculating pump is typically centrifugal and integral with the motor to ensure permanent alignment. This design reduces vibration and noise, which is critical in open-kitchen environments. The motors are typically built for Hobart with inherent thermal protection and grease-packed ball bearings for long-term reliability.Single-Phase Motors: Often capacitor-start, induction-run types.Three-Phase Motors: Squirrel-cage, induction types designed for continuous duty.Sealing Interface: The pump shaft uses a mechanical seal with stainless steel spring components and a carbon-ceramic sealing interface.Mechanical Seal Material ScienceThe choice of carbon-ceramic seals is based on the specific thermodynamic and chemical demands of commercial warewashing.Carbon (Rotating Face): Provides excellent lubricity and self-lubricating properties, allowing the seal to survive momentary dry-run conditions during initial fill.Ceramic (Stationary Seat): Offers superior hardness and resistance to abrasive food particles (such as seeds or bone fragments) that may bypass the scrap screens.Chemical Resistance: These materials are chemically inert to the standard alkaline detergents and chlorine-based sanitizers used in the industry.Water Distribution: Wash Arm and Nozzle EngineeringThe mechanical efficiency of soil removal is governed by the spray pattern and velocity of water exiting the wash arms.Wash Arm DesignModern Hobart machines (LXe, AM16) feature revolving, interchangeable upper and lower wash arms.X-Shaped Arms (AM16): This design provides twice the water coverage of traditional arms, reducing the "blind spots" in the rack.Computational Fluid Dynamics (CFD): Manifolds and nozzles are designed using CFD to ensure that water pressure remains consistent across the entire length of the arm, preventing pressure drops at the furthest nozzles.Anti-Clogging Nozzles: Nozzles are debossed and shaped to shed debris rather than trapping it.Rinse SystemsHobart utilizes the "Opti-Rinse" system in its conveyor and flight-type machines. This system uses specialized nozzles to create a fine, high-velocity spray that sanitizes effectively with significantly less water than traditional flood-type rinse systems. Opti-Rinse nozzles are precision-molded and should never be cleaned with metal tools; Hobart recommends total replacement if the spray pattern becomes degraded.Conveyor Systems: CLeN and CL Series Rack ConveyorsFor operations requiring 200+ racks per hour, Hobart’s rack conveyor machines utilize a continuous drive system to move racks through separate wash, rinse, and sanitization zones.Drive Mechanisms and SafetyThe rack conveyor drive is a critical mechanical assembly, typically powered by a 1/6 HP gearmotor.Rapid Return Drive: A patent-pending mechanism that uses a reciprocating motion to move racks forward.Ball Detent Clutch: This is a safety mechanism integrated into the conveyor drive. If a rack jams (e.g., a utensil falls through the rack into the drive path), the clutch will slip, preventing damage to the motor and drive chain.Table Limit Switch: An external electrical switch located at the end of the clean-dish table. When a rack hits the switch, the conveyor and pumps automatically shut down to prevent racks from falling off the end of the table.Technical Architecture of Conveyor ZonesSectionLengthFunctionTank CapacityPower Scrapper22"Pre-wash soil removal using recirculated water.23 Gal (Combined) Power Wash44" - 66"Primary soil removal zone.23 Gal Power RinseVariableIntermediate rinsing to remove detergent.22 Gal Final Rinse12" - 15"Sanitization zone (180°F or Chemical).N/A (Flow-thru)Flight-Type Engineering: FT1000e SeriesFlight-type machines represent the pinnacle of warewashing engineering, designed for 24/7 operation in high-capacity environments.Conveyor Belt EngineeringThe conveyor belt in the FT1000e series is 30 inches wide, significantly wider than the industry standard. It is constructed with stainless steel side links and tie rods, supporting injection-molded Duraflex flight links. These links are designed to be resilient and easily replaceable if damaged.Pump and Motor ConfigurationsThe FT1000e utilizes a multi-pump architecture, with separate motors for each zone:Prewash Pump: 3-1/2 HP TEFC Motor.Power Wash Pump: 5 HP TEFC Motor.Power Rinse Pump: 5 HP TEFC Motor.Conveyor Motor: 1/3 HP Gearmotor.Ventless Heat-Pump TechnologyThe FT1000e Ventless model is a major engineering breakthrough that eliminates the need for an external exhaust hood.Exhaust Capture: Hot, moisture-laden air is channeled to an internal energy recovery module.Heat Transfer: A refrigeration cycle (utilizing an internal compressor) extracts heat from the exhaust air.Pre-Heating: This captured energy is transferred to the incoming cold water line, raising it from ground temperature to approximately 110°F before it enters the booster heater.Air Conditioning: The chilled air (70°F - 80°F) is then returned to the dishroom, reducing the HVAC load.Electrical Systems and Control ElectronicsHobart's modern electronic architecture is built around highly integrated control boards and user interfaces that facilitate both machine operation and field diagnostics.PCB Terminal Map and Architecture (Centerline Series)The Centerline series (CUH/CUL) provides a representative model of Hobart's contemporary PCB layout.Connector IDEngineering FunctionTechnical DetailsCN5-2 / CN5-3Wash Pump Output120VAC output to 1MTR (Wash Pump Motor).CN7CMain Power InConnects through 1FU fuse to the main transformer.CN9-1Fill ValveEnergizes the fill solenoid for the wash tank.CN9-3Detergent PumpControls the peristaltic detergent dosing motor.CN9-4Rinse Aid PumpControls the peristaltic rinse aid dosing motor.CN9-6Drain PumpEnergizes the automatic pumped drain motor.CN13Booster ProbeThermistor input for 180°F final rinse monitoring.CN14Tank ProbeThermistor input for 150°F wash tank monitoring.CN15-6Detergent SensorInput for low-chemical sensing lance.CN21Door SwitchSafety interlock; circuit must be closed for operation.PRESS1 / 2Pressure TransducersInputs for booster and wash tank water levels.Control Logic and Sequence of OperationsThe microcomputer-timing program follows a strictly defined sequence to ensure NSF compliance.Initial Fill: The control board energizes the fill solenoid (CN9-1). Water level is monitored via pressure sensors (PRESS2).Preheat: Tank heaters are energized until the set-point ($150^\circ F$) is reached, monitored by the tank probe (CN14).Wash Cycle: Upon door closure (CN21), the wash pump motor (CN5) is energized for a timed duration (e.g., 40 seconds).Dwell: A 4-second delay allows the wash manifold to drain and prevents detergent crossover into the rinse cycle.Rinse Cycle: The final rinse pump is energized (or the rinse valve opened) for a timed duration (e.g., 15 seconds), ensuring $180^\circ F$ water is delivered.Sani-Dwell: A final delay ensures that the residual heat on the ware completes the sanitization process.Chemical Injection and Peristaltic Pump MechanicsHobart integrates precise chemical dosing systems to maintain sanitization levels and facilitate drying.Peristaltic Pump DesignThese pumps utilize a rotating assembly to squeeze a flexible tube, creating a vacuum that draws chemicals from external containers.Tubing Materials: Santoprene is used for detergent lines due to its resistance to alkaline breakdown. Sekobril is used for rinse aid lines to handle the specific viscosity of drying agents.Detergent Pump (PR4/PR7): Designed for low-pressure (0.1 bar) delivery of 4 to 7 liters per hour.Rinse Aid Pump (PR1/NPG): Designed for higher pressure (3 bar) to inject against the incoming water line pressure, delivering approximately 1 liter per hour.Dosing Parameters and ConfigurationDosing is configured in the "Manager Menu" of the touchscreen or through physical pots on older control boards.Detergent: Measured in seconds of pump runtime or CCs per cycle.Rinse Aid: Calculated based on water volume to achieve a specific dilution ratio.Sanitizer (Chemical Machines): Must be calibrated to deliver 50 PPM of chlorine. Low-level sensors (S2/S3/S4) provide alerts when chemicals are depleted, and the machine may lock out to prevent non-sanitized cycles.Service Diagnostics and Error Code DatabaseThe Hobart control system provides real-time feedback and diagnostic codes to facilitate rapid repair and minimize downtime.Common Fault Codes and Engineering ResolutionsError CodeDefinitionDiagnostic ProcedureEngineering ResolutionE0, E2, E3, E4Fill ErrorCheck incoming water pressure (15-65 PSI). Inspect fill solenoid for debris.Clean inlet strainer; replace fill valve if coil is open.E5Drain ErrorMonitor drain pump runtime. Check for obstructions in the sump.Clear debris from pump impeller; inspect drain hose for kinks.E7Wash Pump FaultCheck for 3-phase rotation. Inspect wiring harness.Replace thermistor or pressure transducer.Field Configuration ParametersThe "Manager Menu" allows for the customization of the machine's operational envelope.Water Hardness Setting: Used to determine the frequency of the "Delime Notification".Temperature Units: Configurable between °F and °C.Auto-Shutoff (Energy Saver Mode): Programmable delay from 1 to 6 hours before the machine enters a low-power standby state.Wash Cycle Extension: Allows for the adjustment of wash times for "Pot and Pan" modes (2, 4, or 6 minutes).Maintenance Engineering and Preventive ProtocolsTo maintain the high-tolerance requirements of commercial sanitization, Hobart engineering specifies strict maintenance intervals and procedures.Deliming ProcedureChemical deliming is required to remove mineral scale from heating elements and hydraulic lines.Notification: The machine displays a delime alert based on the cycle counter and water hardness settings.Automatic Dosing (AM16/CLeN): Advansys models feature "Complete Delime," which automatically doses delimer into the booster heater and wash tank, ensuring the internal heating elements are fully de-scaled.Manual Inspection: Technicians must verify the condition of the rinse nozzles and heating elements post-delime to ensure all $CaCO_3$ deposits are removed.Filter and Screen ManagementThe filtration system is the first line of defense for the hydraulic pumps.Two-Stage Filtration: Hobart utilizes a primary scrap screen for large solids and a secondary pump intake screen for fine particles.Self-Flushing Strainer: Some models like the LT-1 utilize a self-flushing pump strainer to reduce the frequency of manual cleanings.ASR (Automatic Soil Removal): This system must be inspected daily to ensure the external scrap basket is not overflowing, as this can back up the soil-removal loop.Technical Synthesis and Engineering OutlookHobart’s engineering strategy is characterized by the convergence of mechanical robustness and digital intelligence. The progression from the AM14’s Ni-Resist impellers and electro-mechanical relays to the AM16’s CFD-optimized stainless steel wash systems and WiFi-integrated SmartConnect platforms demonstrates a focus on "Best Lifetime Value". The mechanical core—characterized by 2 HP motors delivering 160 GPM—remains the industry standard for physical soil removal, while the electronic shell provides the precision required to meet increasingly stringent Energy Star 3.0 and NSF sanitization standards.The future of Hobart engineering is clearly directed toward further energy autonomy and predictive service. Technologies such as the ventless heat-pump modules in the FT1000e series and the CLeN-VL models indicate a move toward closed-loop thermal environments that decouple the dishroom from building-wide HVAC and venting requirements. Simultaneously, the transition from float-based level monitoring to pressure transducers and digital telemetry ensures that these machines are no longer passive appliances but integrated data-generating components of the commercial kitchen ecosystem. For the engineering professional, this necessitates a dual mastery of both high-pressure hydraulic mechanics and sophisticated digital control logic to maintain the "Hobart Clean" standard across all equipment generations.

-Technical Engineering Database of Hobart Commercial Warewashing Systems
The structural and functional design of Hobart commercial warewashing equipment is governed by a technical evolution spanning over a century, beginning with the acquisition of the Crescent Washing Machine Company in 1926. The engineering trajectory of these systems has transitioned from purely mechanical assemblies to complex, microprocessor-controlled environments that integrate hydraulic optimization, thermodynamic energy recovery, and sophisticated diagnostic protocols. This database provides a high-density technical analysis of Hobart’s product architecture, encompassing undercounter, door-type, rack conveyor, and flight-type systems, with a focus on engineering specifications and service parameters.Engineering Evolution and Core Architectural PrinciplesThe mechanical integrity of Hobart equipment is primarily founded on the use of 300-series stainless steel for structural frames, wash tanks, and external panels, ensuring resistance to the corrosive effects of high-alkaline detergents and thermal stress. Central to the hydraulic performance is the proprietary vertical pump design, which utilizes a self-draining configuration to prevent the stagnation of water and the accumulation of debris within the impeller housing.Thermodynamic management within these systems is dictated by the Sense-A-Temp™ technology, a thermal interlock system that monitors booster heater temperatures to ensure a minimum 180°F final rinse, as mandated by NSF standards for high-temperature sanitization. In contemporary models, this is augmented by Advansys™ technology, which utilizes energy recovery modules to capture latent heat from vapor and drainage, significantly reducing the total connected electrical load required for the booster heaters.Undercounter Warewashing Systems: Mechanical and Electronic IntegrationThe Hobart undercounter category, currently led by the LXe and LXG series, represents an engineering solution for high-density component placement within a restricted cabinet volume. These units must manage the thermodynamic requirements of high-temperature sanitization while maintaining external cabinet temperatures compatible with under-counter installation in commercial kitchens.LXe Series: Current Production EngineeringThe LXe series is segmented into the LXeH (High Temperature), LXeC (Chemical Sanitizing), and LXeR (Advansys Energy Recovery) variants. The internal architecture of the LXeR models includes a vapor eliminator and a cold-water energy recovery tank, which preheats incoming water from 55°F to approximately 110°F using heat captured from the previous cycle’s steam.Physical and Mechanical SpecificationsThe physical envelope of the LXe series is optimized for both standard and ADA-compliant installations. Standard units feature a 32.5-inch height, while the LXGeR-ADA and LXGePR-ADA models are reduced to 30.5 inches to accommodate modified countertop heights.ParameterLXeH (High Temp)LXeC (Chemical)LXeR (Advansys)Height (Standard)32.5"32.5"32.5"Width23.94"23.94"23.94"Depth25.56"25.56"26.75"Door Opening Height17.0"17.0"17.0"Rack Size20" x 20"20" x 20"20" x 20"Shipping Weight170 lbs170 lbs185 lbsTank Capacity2.9 Gallons2.9 Gallons2.9 GallonsThe hydraulic system utilizes interchangeable, snap-in revolving upper and lower wash and rinse arms. A significant revision occurred at S/N 231176364, where the door assembly design was updated, necessitating different replacement parts for the inner door liner and hinge mechanisms. The LXi models (LXiC, LXiH) were the first to implement the digital display that communicated numeric error codes (E0, E2, E5) which have since been expanded into text-based alerts in the LXe series.Door-Type Warewashers: The AM Series Technical PlatformThe AM series, including models from the legacy AM14 to the current AM16, utilizes a hood-type design for high-volume pass-through or corner installations. The engineering focus in this series is on hydraulic pressure and automated soil management.Hydraulic Systems and Vertical Pump DesignThe heart of the AM series is a Hobart-built, heavy-duty 2 HP wash pump motor. In models like the AM14 and AM15, this motor is vertically mounted and features a Ni-Resist impeller—a high-nickel cast iron alloy chosen for its durability against abrasive detergents and cavitation.Hydraulic MetricAM14 (Legacy)AM15 (Select)AM16 (Current)Pump Motor Power1.0 HP1.5 - 2.0 HP2.0 HPPump Capacity (GPM)165160160Rinse Flow Rate (GPM)2.12.12.1Water Use (Gal/Rack)1.420.740.67Flowing Water Pressure20 ± 5 PSIG20 ± 5 PSIG20 ± 5 PSIGThe AM16 introduced the Automatic Soil Removal (ASR) system, which includes a secondary recirculating pump (8MTR) and a soil pump motor (9MTR). This system actively filters and ejects food particles into an external scrap basket throughout the wash cycle, reducing the frequency of wash water changes and increasing throughput efficiency.Thermal Regulation and Sequence of OperationsThe AM series uses a staged sequence of operations to manage the 5 kW tank heater and the optional 13 kW booster heater. The control board (AI2695) manages these loads based on inputs from temperature probes and pressure transducers.AM16 Sequence of Operations (Electric Heat)The engineering logic for the AM16 is detailed in the service manuals through a series of "K" relay activations :Standby State: Line voltage is present at transformer (1T) primary windings. 120VAC is delivered to the control board at J3.5/J3.7. The display is not lit, but logic power is active.Initial Fill: Upon pressing the "On" key, the board verifies that door switches (LS1, LS2) and the strainer interlock (LS3) are closed. When the full setpoint (0.95V) is reached, the tank heater (K2/HTR1) is energized.Wash Cycle: Contactor 1CON energizes the 2 HP motor for the selected duration (1, 2, 4, or 6 minutes). In hot-water sanitizing mode, a 7-second "Sani-Dwell" occurs after the rinse to ensure thermal compliance.AM14/AM15 Gas Heat LogicFor gas-heated variants, a regulated power immersion tube system is used. A solid-state ignitor board controls the gas valve (SOL3) and provides flame ignition, with a 24V transformer stepping down the control voltage. If the board detects a voltage outside this range during a fill cycle, it will initiate an E0 error and terminate the cycle to prevent a dry-fire of the heating elements.Technical Service Parameters and Maintenance EngineeringTo maintain the operational integrity of these machines, Hobart specifies engineering-level maintenance procedures that go beyond basic cleaning.Scale Management and Booster Guard™The high-temperature environment of the booster heater (180°F+) causes calcium and magnesium to precipitate out of the water quickly. Hobart’s Complete Delime™ with Booster Guard™ is an automated sequence that pumps a precisely metered dose of phosphoric or citric acid into the booster.Notification: The control board monitors water hardness settings (entered via the Manager Menu) and cycle counts to trigger a "Delime Recommended" alert.Procedure: During the delime cycle, the wash pump is disabled, and the delime pump (1PMP) circulates chemical through the booster for 600 seconds. This is followed by three mandatory flush cycles to ensure all acid is removed from the hydraulic path before warewashing resumes.Mechanical Component ReplacementWash Pump Seals: When replacing pump seals on the 2 HP vertical motors, the technician must ensure the carbon-ceramic seal faces are not touched by bare skin, as oils can cause premature failure. The Ni-Resist impeller must be torqued to 35 ft-lbs to maintain the dynamic balance required for 3450 RPM operation.Door Springs: The AM series utilizes spring-counterbalanced doors with nylon guides. Maintenance involves checking the tension of the stainless steel cables. If the hood does not stay in the full-open position, the springs must be adjusted at the rear mounting bracket to prevent the risk of hood-collapse injury.Programming and ConfigurationAccess to the Manager Menu (Code 1001) allows for the adjustment of cycle times, chemical dosage rates, and temperature setpoints.Cycle Selection: The AM16 can be configured for 1, 2, 4, or 6-minute wash cycles. The 4 and 6-minute cycles are NSF Pot and Pan rated and utilize a continuous wash pulse to break down baked-on soils.Chemical Priming: After chemical bottles are replaced, the "Auto-Prime" feature on the LXe and AM16 machines runs the detergent, rinse aid, and sanitizer pumps for a specific duration to purge air from the 1/4" polyethylene supply lines, ensuring consistent chemical concentrations.The technical architecture of Hobart warewashing systems is a testament to iterative engineering, where mechanical durability is enhanced by precise electronic control. From the self-draining vertical pumps to the Advansys energy recovery modules, every component is designed to operate within strict thermodynamic and hydraulic parameters to ensure the highest levels of sanitization and resource efficiency in the commercial food service environment.

Heating & Energy Systems
Engineering Analysis and Technical Database of Hobart Commercial Warewashing SystemsThe engineering of commercial warewashing equipment necessitates a precise convergence of thermodynamic management, hydraulic efficiency, and robust mechanical design to meet rigorous sanitization standards. Hobart Corporation has established a technical lineage characterized by the transition from electromechanical controls to sophisticated microprocessor-driven architectures. This report provides an exhaustive technical database and engineering analysis of Hobart’s product generations, focusing on heating systems, energy recovery mechanisms, and operational parameters across undercounter, door-type, conveyor, and flight-type platforms.Thermal Engineering and Sanitization ArchitectureThe central engineering objective in Hobart warewashing systems is the maintenance of high-temperature sanitization through controlled thermal input. The equipment must elevate incoming water to a minimum of $180^{\circ}F$ ($82.2^{\circ}C$) for the final rinse cycle to comply with NSF/ANSI standards for high-temperature machines. This is achieved through a combination of tank heaters for wash water maintenance and booster heaters for the final rinse temperature rise.Heating Methodologies and SpecificationsHobart employs three primary heating methods across its product lines: electric resistance, steam heat exchange, and gas immersion. The selection of heating type significantly influences the electrical connected load and site utility requirements.Electric Resistance HeatingElectric immersion elements are the standard for most undercounter and door-type models. In the LXe series, the electric tank heater is rated at $1.8\text{ kW}$, while the booster heater for high-temperature models (LXeH) is rated at $4.9\text{ kW}$ to achieve a $70^{\circ}F$ rise. Larger units, such as the AM16 series, utilize a $5\text{ kW}$ wash tank heater and a $13\text{ kW}$ booster heater.Model CategoryWash Tank Heat (Electric)Booster Heat (Electric)Total Connected Load (Approx. 208V/3PH)LXeR (Undercounter)$1.8\text{ kW}$$4.9\text{ kW}$$30.5\text{ Amps}$ (1PH)AM16 (Door-Type)$5\text{ kW}$$13\text{ kW}$$43.9\text{ Amps}$CLPS66eN (Conveyor)$15\text{ kW}$$30\text{ kW}$$100.5\text{ Amps}$FT1000i (Flight-Type)$56\text{ kW}$ (Total Tank)$26\text{ kW}$$150-450\text{ Amps}$Steam Heat Exchange SystemsSteam systems are utilized in high-volume conveyor and flight-type machines. These systems employ steam coils or injectors to transfer heat from a facility’s boiler system. In the CLPS66eN conveyor series, steam coils consume $65\text{ lbs/hr}$ at $10$ to $50\text{ PSI}$ flowing pressure. The condensate return requires a $10\text{ PSI}$ minimum differential pressure across the steam trap to ensure the removal of latent heat and prevent water hammer.Gas Immersion HeatingLegacy and current door-type models, such as the AM14 and AM15, offer gas-fired options. These units utilize an immersion tube burner system with a regulated power burner. The AM14C, for instance, features a gas burner rated at $25,000\text{ BTU/hr}$ with a solid-state ignitor board for automatic ignition and flame monitoring.Sense-A-Temp™ Booster TechnologyA critical mechanical component is the Sense-A-Temp™ booster heater. This system is engineered to interlock the rinse cycle with the booster temperature, ensuring that the machine does not enter the final rinse phase until the water reaches the required $180^{\circ}F$. The booster is typically constructed of stainless steel and incorporates a high-limit protector (e.g., Hobart part 00-271123) to prevent dry-firing and overheating.Energy Recovery and Thermodynamic OptimizationA major area of engineering innovation in Hobart systems is the recapture of waste energy. Modern units such as the Advansys™ models utilize thermodynamic principles to reduce the total energy input required for water heating.Advansys™ Energy Recovery and Steam EliminationThe Advansys™ system, integrated into LXeR and LXnR undercounter models, is a ventless technology that eliminates the need for an external ventilation hood. The system operates via a condensing cycle at the end of the wash process.Vapor Capture: Steam and hot water vapor are extracted from the chamber.Heat Exchange: The vapor is passed over a condenser coil filled with incoming cold water.Phase Change: The steam condenses into water droplets, releasing its latent heat of vaporization ($L_v \approx 2260\text{ kJ/kg}$) to the incoming water.Preheating: Incoming water is elevated from $55^{\circ}F$ to approximately $140^{\circ}F$ before entering the booster heater.This process reduces the booster heater's energy consumption by approximately $42\%$ and improves the kitchen's room climate by preventing humidity release.Drain Water Energy Recovery (DWER)In high-capacity conveyor models like the CLeN series, DWER technology captures sensible heat from the outgoing drain water. As hot wash water is discharged (typically at $150^{\circ}F$), it passes through a heat exchanger where it preheats incoming rinse water. Engineering data indicates this reduces rinse water heating energy by up to $20\%$ and reduces the amount of cold water required for drain water tempering (to meet plumbing codes) by up to $90\%$.Climate-Pro and Exhaust Heat RecoveryThe PREMAX and Profi flight-type series utilize the Climate-Pro energy system, which focuses on exhaust air regeneration. The system captures $100\%$ of the thermal energy in the exhaust and returns it to the rinse process, saving up to $22\text{ kWh}$ per hour. The exhaust heat recovery unit heats incoming fresh water using $60^{\circ}C$ ($140^{\circ}F$) waste steam, returning the energy via a high-efficiency heat exchanger.Mechanical and Hydraulic System ParametersThe hydraulic architecture of a Hobart dishwasher must ensure high-velocity impingement while minimizing water volume. This balance is managed through pump design, nozzle geometry, and filtration systems.Pump and Motor SpecificationsHobart utilizes heavy-duty motors, typically Totally Enclosed Fan Cooled (TEFC), with inherent thermal protection and grease-packed ball bearings.Machine TypeModelWash Pump HPPump Capacity (GPM)Motor ProtectionUndercounterLXeR$0.85$Not SpecifiedInternal ThermalDoor-TypeAM14$1.0$Not SpecifiedMagnetic SwitchDoor-TypeAM16$2.0$Not SpecifiedSolid StateConveyorC44A$2.0$ (approx.)$151$ (Weir Test)Thermal OverloadFlight-TypeFT1000i$4.0$ (Pre-Wash)Not SpecifiedTEFCThe wash pumps are designed to be self-draining to prevent bacterial growth and cross-contamination between cycles. The impellers are typically stainless steel to resist cavitation and chemical corrosion.Hydraulic Control and Opti-Rinse™Modern Hobart conveyor systems utilize the Opti-Rinse™ system, which optimizes the final rinse spray pattern. By using larger droplets and a specifically engineered nozzle angle, the system achieves effective sanitization with a significantly reduced water flow rate.Final Rinse Flow Rate (C44A): $1.83\text{ GPM}$ at $15\text{ PSI}$.Final Rinse Flow Rate (FT1000i): $1.3\text{ GPM}$ (pumped rinse).Water Usage (LXeR): $0.62\text{ gallons per rack}$.Hydraulic management also includes automatic soil removal (ASR) systems. ASR uses a dedicated pump to extract food soil from the wash tank and deposit it into an external scrap basket, maintaining wash water quality and reducing the need for tank refills.Undercounter Dishwasher Engineering (LX and LXe Series)The undercounter series has evolved from the LXi (legacy) to the LXe (current) and LXn (next generation) architectures. Each revision has focused on increasing the loading height and reducing water consumption.Technical Specifications: LXe SeriesThe LXe series is characterized by a $17\text{-inch}$ door opening, allowing for the washing of $18" \times 26"$ bun pans.SpecificationLXeH (High Temp)LXeC (Low Temp)LXeR (Advansys)Racks Per Hour$32$$34$$30$ (Light) / $24$ (Normal) / $13$ (Heavy)Water Use (Gal/Rack)$0.74$$0.74$$0.62$Tank Capacity$2.9\text{ Gal}$$2.9\text{ Gal}$$2.9\text{ Gal}$Wash Temp$150^{\circ}F$$120^{\circ}F$$150^{\circ}F$Rinse Temp$180^{\circ}F$$120^{\circ}F$$180^{\circ}F$Shipping Weight$210\text{ lbs}$$190\text{ lbs}$$185\text{ lbs}$Mechanical Components and LayoutThe LXe series employs a deep-drawn stainless steel tank to eliminate welded seams where bacteria or debris could accumulate. A key engineering difference in the LXe series is the introduction of the digital Smart Display, which replaced the simpler microcomputer interface of the LXi and provides detailed diagnostic text instead of simple numeric codes.Door-Type Series Engineering (AM12, AM14, AM15, AM16)Door-type machines are designed for high-frequency operation in small to medium-sized kitchens. The AM series has undergone continuous engineering refinement to improve motor torque and thermal stability.AM16 Technical ArchitectureThe AM16 is the current production model, featuring a pillarless vertical door opening of $18\text{ inches}$. The machine is built on a $16\text{-gauge}$ welded stainless steel frame and tank.Electrical Parameter208-240V / 60Hz / 3PH208-240V / 60Hz / 1PH480V / 60Hz / 3PHRated Amps$37.4\text{A} - 43.9\text{A}$$67.4\text{A} - 79.2\text{A}$$21.5\text{A}$Min. Circuit Ampacity$50\text{A} - 60\text{A}$$85\text{A} - 100\text{A}$$30\text{A}$Max. Protective Device$50\text{A} - 60\text{A}$$85\text{A} - 100\text{A}$$30\text{A}$The AM16 utilizes a dual-level sanitization monitoring system. For high-temperature mode, the machine monitors the Sense-A-Temp™ booster. For low-temperature chemical mode, the machine is field-convertible and relies on an injection system for sodium hypochlorite.Legacy AM12 and AM14 ParametersThe AM12 and AM14 series were characterized by their timed fill and electromechanical interlocks. The AM12 used a manual timed fill switch that required $2\text{ minutes and } 18\text{ seconds}$ to complete. Both models featured a "ratio fill" method to ensure the correct water volume in the wash tank relative to the final rinse.The AM14 introduced a more refined microcomputer control system and was constructed with an arc-welded #16-gauge stainless steel tank. It featured a $1\text{ HP}$ pump and was capable of $52$ to $80$ racks per hour depending on the operating mode.Conveyor and Flight-Type Industrial SystemsHigh-volume systems are engineered for continuous duty and are categorized by their rack-advance (conveyor) or peg-link (flight-type) mechanisms.CL and CLe Series Conveyor EngineeringThe CL (Base) and CLe (Enhanced) models are rack conveyor systems capable of processing up to $202$ racks per hour in a $44\text{-inch}$ frame. The engineering of the CLeN series (the latest revision) includes capless wash arms to prevent the loss of small mechanical parts and simplify daily cleaning protocols.CLPS66eN (Steam Heat) Operational ParametersThe CLPS66eN is a $66\text{-inch}$ rack conveyor with an integrated power scrapper.Steam Heating Consumption: $65\text{ lbs/hr}$ for the tank; $60\text{ lbs/hr}$ for the optional steam booster.Electrical Configuration: Dual-point connection is standard for $208\text{V}$ and $240\text{V}$ units to separate motor/control loads from the high-amperage $30\text{ kW}$ booster.Mechanical Drive: Rapid-return conveyor drive mechanism with a ball detent clutch to protect the motor from mechanical jams.Flight-Type Systems: FT1000i and FT1000eFlight-type machines are the pinnacle of Hobart’s engineering, intended for airports, hospitals, and large institutions.Engineering ParameterFT1000i (Airline)FT1000e (Base)Conveyor Width$30\text{ inches}$$30\text{ inches}$Throughput (Dishes/Hr)$14,316$Not SpecifiedFinal Rinse Consumption$78\text{ GPH}$$58\text{ GPH}$ (approx.)Total Tank Heat (Electric)$56\text{ kW}$$39\text{ kW}$ (approx.)Connected Load (480V)Varies$150\text{ Amps}$The FT1000i Airline model is specifically designed for the dimensional requirements of airline catering trays, featuring a $22\text{-inch}$ chamber height and $31\text{-inch}$ access panels on the power wash chambers. Modern Hobart machines feature an "Auto-Delime" cycle.Complete Delime™ with Booster Guard™: In CL and LX Advansys models, the machine automatically pumps deliming chemicals into both the wash tank and the booster heater.Manager Notification: The control system tracks cycle counts and displays a "Delime Recommended" message. Clearing this message requires entering Manager Mode and resetting the delime timer.Chemical Pumps: LXe models feature "Auto-Prime" chemical pumps for detergent, rinse-aid, and sanitizer, reducing the manual labor required during chemical changes.Component Serviceability and Parts DataStandard service tasks involve the replacement of wear items such as o-rings, shaft seals, and probe assemblies.ComponentPart NumberEngineering FunctionFloat Switch00-289121Low water protection for heating elements.Shaft Seal00-274227-00006Prevents leakage from the wash pump motor.Vacuum Breaker00-292909-00003Prevents backflow of wash water into the potable supply.Pressure Gauge00-918429Monitors flowing water pressure ($0-60\text{ PSI}$).High Limit Protector00-271123Thermal safety cutoff for the booster heater.Field Installation EngineeringInstallation must adhere to specific utility and mechanical parameters to ensure the machine operates within its design envelope.Water Hardness: Recommended at $2-6\text{ grains per gallon}$ ($3\text{ grains}$ or less for CL series).Flowing Pressure: Must be maintained at $15-65\text{ PSIG}$ ($20 \pm 5\text{ PSIG}$ for CL and AM series).
